COMPREHENSIVE DEVELOPMENT PLAN (CDP) 2016-2023 RELIGIOUS AND CULTURAL TOURISM CITY GOVERNMENT OF SAN PEDRO, LAGUNA SITUATIONAL ANALYSIS

![image 122](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile122.png)

### Religious and Cultural Tourism

The city religious and tourism industry has not yet been fully developed to be at par with national and international standards, although it has the potential to, particularly the Miraculous Jesus Nazareno (Lolo Uweng) Shrine in Barangay Landayan. It is perennially visited by thousands of Christian pilgrims every Friday especially during the Lenten Season. From the number of economic activities it generates, it provides substantial employment and additional income to the informal sector.

On the whole, tourism related services are not progressing fast for lack of a master plan involving tourist spot development. These spots are the Laurel Museum, Risen Christ Statue, St. Peter the Apostle Statue in Laguna Lake, and the Lolo Uweng Shrine.

Promoting native delicacies, local handicraft and local festivals should also be intensified. A high end Korean owned golf club – KC Filipinas – situated at the upland is being patronized by local and foreign golf players.

San Pedro intends to revive historical milestones, native products and services that originated from years ago, like the duck raising and balut-making industry, native food products from the old industries like sweets making, roast duck, and sampaguita flower planting, harvesting and lei making as these could serve as attractions for local and foreign tourists. Moreover, these could stimulate the development of the local micro, small and medium enterprises with technical assistance from Department of Science and Technology (DOST).

![image 123](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile123.png)

### Human and Social Development

![image 124](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile124.png)

#### Key Issues and Concerns

###### 1.6.1.1 Field Health Services

- a. Increasing Number of People Getting Lifestyle Acquired Diseases
- b. Low compliance in terms of completion in infant immunization, completing the pre natal care, others
- c. Deficiency in the Number of Health Care Provider
- d. Need for new City Plantilla for health to include SPJLAEH, GALIC and RHU I & II
- e. Need for a Permanent Rural Health Unit I Facility and Purchase of Lot for the Health Facility Building
- f. Non Compliance with the Revised DOH Guidelines on Health Infrastructure Facility
- g. High Prevalence of Animal Bites particularly Dogs
- h. High Prevalence of Tuberculosis
- i. Increasing Population and low impact of Family Planning Program
- j. GALIC – Need for Infrastructure Renovation of the Lying In Compliance with the new DOH Guidelines to be licensed as Primary Health Care Facility Category of Infirmary
- k. Need for Diagnostic Services for the people in the Upper Villages such as laboratory and X Ray facility
- l. High Prevalence of HIV-AIDS with San Pedro ranking No. 2 in the Province of Laguna
- m. SPJLAEH – Increasing Number of Out Patients requiring medical services, limited space for OPD
- n. Congestion of Hospital Area due to large number of patients
- o. Inadequate Number of Physicians that would handle OPD, ER, Ward and OB unit
- p. High Cost of Hospital Services to include PS, MOOE and Capital Expenditures
- q. Increasing Number of different diseases ranging from mild to severe


###### 1.6.1.2 Veterinary Public Health

- a. Need for a City Veterinary Office
- b. Insufficient number of veterinarians and meat inspectors
- c. Need for medicines like de-wormer, vitamins) and supplies
- d. Lack of “AA” Slaughterhouse


###### 1.6.1.3 Sanitation Office

- a. Low awareness on WASH (Water, Sanitation & Hygiene) Program
- b. Poor solid waste management
- c. Inadequate number of health personnel for Sanitation Unit


###### 1.6.1.4 Nutrition Office

- a. Poor implementation of Exclusive Breastfeeding Program
- b. Increasing number of patient with diet related diseases
- c. Declining nutritional status of young child


- d. Lack of access to nutritious / healthy food
- e. Non-functional City Nutrition Council
- f. Non-creation of City Nutrition Cluster and Barangay Nutrition Cluster
- g. Lack of proper filing BNS data base
- h. Inadequate training of Barangays Nutrition Scholars


###### 1.6.1.5 Sector Description

Health Status. The general level of health conditions of San Pedro can be described as satisfactory and acceptable levels in terms of morbidity and mortality in relation to the total population. The crude birth rate per 1,000 populations in 2015 is 13.78 as compared to 23.13 percent in 2010.Crude death rate per 1,000 populations in 2015 is 2.50 percent as compared to 4.38 percent in2010.

The leading cause of morbidity in 2010 is Asthma while the 2015 figures indicate leading morbidity diseases were URTI and influenza. Influenza which is No. 7 in 2010 went up to No. 1 in 2015. The repetitive occurrence of yearly seasonal influenza epidemics is due to the fact that influenza viruses are continuously changing antigenically. Influenza virus is also easily transmitted from person to person. Other leading causes of morbidity which were prevalent in both 2010 and 2015 data are UTI and hypertension

Health Care Provision and Utilization. The health programs are aimed at reducing mortality and morbidity especially the vulnerable groups like children, women and the elderly. The following graph and data shows the accomplishment by program.

Maternal Care Program. Provision of free services to all women before, during and after pregnancy which includes the dimensions in family planning, preconception, prenatal and postnatal services to reduce maternal mortality as well as safe pregnancy and delivery and parenting in all barangay of City of San Pedro. Free Pap’s Smear, Visual Inspection Using Acetic Acid (VIA) and Breast Cancer Awareness are also included in the Program as well as Provision of Maternal Care Package under PhilHealth.

###### Table 27. Pregnant Women under Maternal Care Program

|YEAR|PREGNANT WOMEN|
|---|---|
|2015|630|
|2016|622|


Child Care Program. Improving the health of eligible population against vaccine preventable disease thru free immunization. This includes routine vaccination of: Tuberculosis, Diphtheria, Pertussis, Tetanus, Poliomyelitis, and Measles. Master listing and catch-up immunization is also conducted to immunize defaulters to increase herd immunity in the community. Free Consultation and recording and monitoring of children under 5 years of age and sick children are also part of Child Care as well as Mass immunization and School Based Immunization program.

###### Table 28. Recipients of Child Care Program

|YEAR|NUMBER OF CHILDREN|
|---|---|
|2015|4,588|
|2016|4,966|


- SOURCE: RHU-1,CITY OF SAN PEDRO 2015

Elderly Program. Maintaining the health of aging population thru provision of Free Anti-Hypertensive and Diabetes prescribed drugs as well as free immunization against Pneumonia and Influenza. Free Zumba in every barangay enjoining the elderly is also conducted to decrease lifestyle acquired diseases. Awareness is also conducted regarding Prostate Cancer as well as free consultation and screening of diabetes and hypertension.

Table 29. Vaccine Recipients

#### Basic Education

###### 1.6.3.1 Key Issues and Concerns

- 1. No buildable space in Langgam and Bagong Silang Elementary School
- 2. Some pupils’ residences are far from school
- 3. Poor academic performance of the pupils in Math, English and Science
- 4. Large number of non-readers
- 5. Low performance in achievement test
- 6. Poor Communication system during typhoons, fire and earthquakes
- 7. Lack of computer sets, poor computer literacy and slow connection
- 8. Bullying among school children
- 9. Cases of violation against children
- 10. Meager knowledge on the implementation of GAD program
- 11. Distance of teachers’ residences to their teaching assignment
- 12. Overcrowded house or habitation of student families


###### 1.6.3.2 Sectoral Description

Education sector plays a vital role in the socio-economic development of the city. Necessary knowledge, skills and training can be provided by different education institutions both public and private, from primary to higher institutions. Relative to the increasing population of the City, more education facilities are indispensable. This is where Public-Private partnerships (PPP) enter.

Efficiency of the education programs are reflected on the succeeding indicators as reflected on the data given. There’s almost 100% completion rate of elementary from school year 20122013 to 2014-2015 which means that those who entered in the primary level finished their course on the prescribed period (table 16).

The dropout rate is very marginal ranging from 0.13 to 1.5 percent as shown in table17. Most of the schools in San Pedro is just a walk away from residences of students, making it accessible to all. Nevertheless, drop outs among high school students due to everyday expenses of going to school, lack of interest and parental responsibility and guidance.

In table 18, it was evident that there was a fluctuating ratio among teachers, facilities and students, to cater the needs for education. Though there are also many private schools in San Pedro, not everyone can afford to enroll thus, the influx of students is in the public schools.

This is also one of the effects not only by the growing population nevertheless the impact of the rising urbanization in the City, where people legally and illegally migrate in the area.

Among the intervention programs undertaken by DepEd District Office to meet pupil problems are the following:

- 1. School Based Feeding Program for malnourished or severely wasted pupils.
- 2. Expanded Universal Medical and Dental Checkup.
- 3. Oral Health Care/Bright Smile Program.
- 4. Child Friendly School.
- 5. Pre School Program.
- 6. Head Start Program for the Gifted.
- 7. Special Education Program.
- 8. GAD and Violence against Women and Children.
- 9. Dropout Intervention/Reduction Program.


###### Private Schools

San Pedro has quite a number of tertiary level private schools offering degree programs such as San Pedro School of Business, St. Louis Anne, among others, while a big number of student residents are enrolled in big schools in Metro Manila. Local colleges are performing well in licensure examinations such as CPAs as advertised by them. They have good faculty line up, facilities and equipment conforming to the standards of the Commission on Higher Education (CHED).

School year 2015-2016 marks the beginning of the K-12 program, where more challenges are about to be encountered by the City, education as one of the sectors’ basic needs must be given a priority especially of the urbanizing City, to ensure its populace productivity in the community.

![image 129](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile129.png)

#### Technical and Vocational Education

###### 1.6.4.1 Key Issues and Concerns

- 1. Need for the constant retooling of school curriculum, facilities and teacher retraining in coordination with the industry.
- 2. Need to address quality of training education in terms of skills competency.
- 3. Placement and Job Opportunities for Graduates in the labor market.
- 4. TESDA assistance in Scholarship Vouchers for preferred courses are very limited.
- 5. Financial problems of Trainees lead to dropouts.
- 6. Opportunities of the IT BPO industry for employment must be tapped.


###### San Pedro Technical Institute

San Pedro Technological Institute (SPTI) is a Technical-Vocational school funded by the City Government of San Pedro, Laguna under the Education Component of San Pedro Roadmap 2020, offering Technical – Vocational courses recognized by the Technical Education and Skills Development Authority.

###### Private Technical Vocational Schools

There are a few number of private technical vocational schools like Malasaga Technical Institute offering non degree programs in electronics, metal and mechanical works, information technology, among others. They provide employable and trainable middle level manpower required by the local industry although many graduates tend to apply for overseas employment and work outside of the City.

#### Tertiary Education

###### 1.6.5.1 Key Issues and Concerns

- 1. The IT BPO service industry both voice and non-voice have the biggest potential to employ the graduating students of San Pedro although there are no IT BPO companies operating in the City unlike in Santa Rosa and Muntinlupa
- 2. Private tertiary level private schools in San Pedro maybe producing quality employable graduates but they work outside of San Pedro which is not contributing positively to the local economy
- 3. There is a need to conduct Labor Market Study to determine the professionals and skills needed by the local economy and business establishments
- 4. The students are beset with financial instability resulting in dropping out from their higher education courses
- 5. The graduating students to be employable and acceptable to the employers must be imbued with positive work ethics, attitudes and values as part of the curricular programs of the schools
- 6. The PESO of San Pedro should conduct regular job fairs and marketing strategies to enable job seekers find employment in business companies
- 7. The students need to be introduced in entrepreneurial courses for self-employment
- 8. The PUP in San Pedro has very limited capacity in terms of accommodating students for enrollment in various courses
- 9. There is a need for coordination between the DEPED and PUP in the placements of graduating high school students of San Pedro
- 10. San Pedro needs to start the establishment of a State Polytechnic College through the conduct of feasibility study showing its financial and technical viability, operation and management


###### Polytechnic University of the Philippines – San Pedro Campus

Polytechnic University of the Philippines (PUP) San Pedro Campus was established on April 9, 2012 as an Open University center with an initial offering of BS in Entrepreneurial Management. In the past 12 years, PUP pursued its 8 Point agenda namely:

- a. Pursuing Academic Excellence through Disciplinary Integrity
- b. Embedding a Culture of Research
- c. Insuring Transparency and Participation in Giving Reward and Sanctions
- d. Modernizing and Upgrading of Physical Facilities, Equipment, Library and Campus
- e. Academic Freedom
- f. Institutionalizing Civil Society Engagement and Involved Extension Service Program
- g. Assuring Transparency in Fiscal Responsibility
- h. Assessing Institutional Processes and Reviewing Critically and Rationally the Organization


###### The curricular offerings are as follows:

- a. BS in Business Administration major in Marketing Management and Human Resource Development
- b. BS in Accountancy
- c. BS in Entrepreneurship
- d. BS in Information Technology
- e. BS in Secondary Education major in English and Mathematics

The profile of the students/graduates are as follows:

- 1. Civil Status: 70.37 percent Single; 29.63 percent Married
- 2. Degree Earned: 100 percent are BSEM
- 3. Reasons for taking Bachelor’s Degree: 35 percent -affordability; 30 percent -course availability; 13 percent - compensation; 7 percent career development; others – prestige, passion, role model, parent’s influence and good grades
- 4. 100 percent of graduates passed the Civil Service Examination (Professional Level)
- 5. First job after College: 55 percent (less than 6 months); 25 percent (2 years up); 9 percent (6 months to 1 year); 4 percent (1 year to less than 2 years); 4 percent (never been employed)
- 6. Reasons for Unemployment: 80 percent health reasons; 20 percent no job opportunity
- 7. Job Status: 65 percent regular/permanent; 30.43 percent casual/contractual; 3 percent freelance
- 8. Occupation: 80 percent- non managerial; 13.36 percent- manager; 4.54 percent – supervisor
- 9. Gross Monthly Income: P10,000 to 20,000 - 62 percent; P30,000- 19 percent; P20,000 to 30,000- 13.64 percent
- 10. Place of Work: 81 percent – Local; 18.18 percent- Abroad
- 11. Nature of Industry: 16.67 percent Services; 16 percent Banking/Finance; 12.5 percent Manufacturing; 12.5 percent Telecommunication; 8.3 percent Motor Industry; 8.3 percent Computer/Software; Others in academics, electronics, machinery, packaging and BPO
- 12. Duration in Present Job: 2 to 4 years 40 percent; less than 2 years 33 percent; 4 years up 27.27 percent
- 13. Relevance of Course and Job: yes 41 percent; No 42.86 percent
- 14. Competencies Learned in College: 35 percent Communication Skills; 26.47 percent Human Relation Skills; 17.65 percent Entrepreneurial Skills 17.65 percent; 11.7 percent Problem Solving Skills; 8.82 percent Information Technology Skills


###### Performance in Licensure Examination

- 1. Licensure Examination for Teachers Secondary is above the national average (24 to 39 percent) for the past seven years (2008-2014); 55 percent passers in 2015
- 2. CPA Board Examination- 75.73 percent Passing Rate in October 2015


Maintaining the campus in the city is quite expensive, and only a few students from San Pedro are being catered by the state college as discussed in the given data. A plan to establish a city college to accommodate more students from San Pedro is now in the process of consultations and deliberations among different sectors. Nevertheless, budget is not an issue to debate; it’s the area where it should be built.

![image 131](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile131.png)

#### Public Order and Safety

- 1.6.6.1 Key Issues and Concerns - Philippine National Police (PNP)


- 1. City Police Station Land Area, Building and Facilities

-  Ideal Land Area for a City Police Station: 1000sq.mtrs. Current Land Area: 310 sq. mtrs.
-  Building Construction not suited to cater the needs of the PNP personnel and the customers (community)
-  No parking space for visitors
-  No parking space for customers
-  Insufficient office room per section
-  Temporary lock-up cell is congested (59 inmates where 48males; 11females) with only one (1) comfort room
-  Quality Service Lane cannot be implemented


- 2. Insufficient number of personnel

-  Ideal personnel to population ratio 1:1000 (323,352)
-  Current personnel 131 or 1:2468 ratio
-  Ideal no. of personnel:323
-  By 2020:352 (352,394)


- 3. Insufficient no of mobile patrol is 5
- 4. Insufficient no of Firearms (Total No of Personnel: 131 and total number of personnel without firearms arm 20)
- 5. Insufficient no. of Handheld Radios

 Total No of Personnel with radio is 29 (Brgy. Tanods, POSO, TRU, SPARCU, CDRMMC should have one frequency)

- 6. Insufficient Street Lights and unserviceable CCTV with no monitor installed in the station


- 8. Insufficient Traffic Signages and Undisciplined public transport/pedestrian
- 9. Lack of Search and Rescue Equipment

###### 1.6.6.3 Key Issues and Concerns - Bureau of Fire Protection (BFP)

- 1. Need for additional substation for Barangay Narra.
- 2. Need for additional fire trucks preferably (Mini fire trucks/penetrators) to meet actual number 1 fire truck per every 20,000 population there should be 16 fire truck. Current: 1 BFP / 4 CFAU fire trucks available with AOR
- 3. Need for additional organic personnel for every 20,000 population; 1 fireman need of additional 166 personnel-Current: 16 BFP personnel and 17 Local personnel.
- 4. Unserviceable hydrants because of low water pressure.
- 5. Lack of existing water service providers.
- 6. Lack of vehicles that can be used for building fire safety inspection.
- 7. Obstruction of Informal Settler Communities.
- 8. Lack of rescue equipment Fire hydrants sometimes do not fit w/ hose equipment.

#### Climate Change and Disaster Risk Mitigation

- 1.6.8.1 Key Issues and Concerns

- 1. River System vulnerable to flood water overflow during heavy rains, lack of easements, soil erosion and siltation
- 2. Possible ground subsidence and salt water intrusion aggravating the earthquake fault line caused by unregulated extraction of ground water
- 3. Lakeshore communities highly vulnerable to the rise of Laguna Lake water reaching 14 meters during the typhoon season
- 4. Storm water, drainage and flood control infrastructure inadequate to address flash flooding, soil erosion and landslide
- 5. Need to continue capacity building through training and IEC to climate change vulnerable communities to make them disaster resilient
- 6. Need to strengthen a community based early warning and effective communication system using the resources of the private sector and non-government organizations
- 7. Need to procure new disaster mitigation equipment like all-weather monitoring system of humidity, barometer, wind speed and direction, flood forecasting, others


- 1.6.8.2 Sectoral Description


Climate Change Adaptation

Climate Change will have impacts on the current and future growth and development of San Pedro. Increased temperature will result in ground water depletion and drought resulting in water shortages, limited urban agricultural production, exaggerated urban heat island effects and increased energy demands. Increased precipitation will result in increased risk of flooding with consequences in damage to infrastructure and properties, interruption in food supply network, disruption of livelihood and city economics, displacement of population built on hazardous areas and increased incidence of water borne diseases.

In view of these, development should be prevented in high hazard areas where possible but where development cannot be prevented, we can minimize land use intensity, buildings value and occupancy. Where development occurs, we can mitigate risks through urban design, site planning and building construction. For communities already built up, we can protect life and existing development through evacuation plans, redevelopment and retrofitting. Future development should not disturb protective environmental systems and should not create conditions that contribute to risks.

San Pedro needs to plan for hazard prone areas in the Laguna Lakeshore, river systems, and flash flood prone interior areas. In the Lakeshore, there is a need to preserve open areas (where it exists) control growing areas (density controls and lakeshore, easements), ensure developments in flood resilient (site and building standards) study implications of possible infrastructure/engineering interventions (road dike, land raising), study areas for possible redevelopment and resettlement planning.

San Pedro should continue the widening and dredging of the waterways, retrofitting of bridges, improve the urban drainage system intakes and outfalls and explore the establishment of detention ponds and implement best practices on rain water harvesting, others. The retention of specific green areas for open spaces and parks in every Barangays are needed as buffer for climate change adaptation and disaster risk mitigation.

San Pedro should attend to the monitoring and regulation in the extraction of ground water, promotion of green technology for energy efficiency and water conservation in the construction of houses, office buildings and factory buildings. More efficient solid waste management system other than the MRF such as cutting edge technology on waste to energy that needs no sanitary landfill should be explored. Disaster rescue, evacuation and relief assistance should be done for the people affected by flooding, landslide and earthquakes seismic movements including the establishment of permanent evacuation centers, capacity building and conduct of drills and exercises at the Barangay level as well as in schools, public and private establishments.

Areas Vulnerable to Disaster

- 1. Affected by erosions/landslide
- 2. Affected by lake overflow along the lakeshore
- 3. Affected by movement of Earthquake Fault line


![image 134](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile134.png)

#### Housing and Resettlement

###### 1.6.9.1 Key Issues and Concerns

- 1. Need to prioritize the awarding of housing beneficiaries in order to lessen the number of housing applicants in need and at the same time lessen if not resolved our housing backlog.
- 2. The housing awardees are not monitored properly resulting in the buying - selling of rights for the reasons that they have either no source of income at the site or very far from their jobs. In effect, they forced themselves to go back to illegal settling or where they came from.
- 3. Need for City Housing Project for employees and marginalized sector of our city. We have no housing project to offer or provide in spite of many housing applicants who are either renters, sharers or extended families whom are wanting to have housing units of their own.
- 4. Lack of government properties and public domain areas for resettlement project. Most of the available lots or properties are privately owned and due to low appraisal value of lot, private landowners refused to sell their lots to the government and they usually accommodate private developer for a much higher price.
- 5. Need to allocate public funds for land development and dwelling construction. The budget needed for housing keeps on increasing as long as the housing needs are not yet addressed.
- 6. Inability to control the proliferation of informal settlers in the barangay level that makes resettlement and relocation extremely needed. Barangay is not aware of the influx of informal settlers in some of their areas and the result is the growing number of IFS which need to resettled and relocated.
- 7. Appreciating values of agricultural land, a challenge to socialized housing gap.


###### 1.6.9.2 Description of the Sector

The City of San Pedro became the Dormitory City as it serves as the gateway or an easy access to Metro Manila. The number of subdivisions in different barangays in San Pedro is an evidence of the influx of migrants coming from other neighboring cities or municipalities who wants to live here. At present, there are more than one hundred and twenty-eight (128) subdivisions in the 2,260 hectares land area of the City of San Pedro.

The Upper Barangays of Magsaysay, Narra, Riverside, Bagong Silang, Laram, United Bayanihan, Langgam, Estrella and United Better Living were considered as resettlement area since 1968. For so many decades, these barangays are being developed and lots of different subdivisions were constructed and still continuing. The Barangays of San Vicente and San Antonio are the two barangays where most of the subdivisions are located.

Based on the inventory conducted by City Urban Development and Housing Office last December, 2014, there are 11, 300 informal settlers in our city and continuously increasing. These informal settlers occupy and built their houses/ shanties in danger areas like creeks/esteros, lakeshore, under the bridge, road lots and railways; government lots like open spaces and under NAPOCOR line; and even private lots. They are those who need to be relocated and resettled to havedecent, safe and affordable housing of their own.

Due to increasing number of informal settlers and at the same time, no enough government lots for housing and resettlement in the City of San Pedro, it is but necessary to have a vertical housing project with the assistance of National Agencies and Non-Government Organizations

such as National Housing Authority (NHA), Housing and Urban Development Coordinating Council (HUDCC), Social Housing Finance Corp. (SHFC), Housing andLand Use Regulatory Board (HLURB), Pag-IBIG Funds, GawadKalinga, Habitat for Humanities, and other entities related to housing.

###### 1.6.9.3 Current Housing Situation

As a dormitory city to Metro Manila, San Pedro has about a hundred residential subdivisions in its 2,260 hectares land area. Majority of the households own the house and lot they occupy while the rest are just renting or rent free occupants. Almost all of the housing units are made of strong materials like concrete, brick, stone and wood.

Electricity is adequately provided by Meralco to all residents. The SP Water District supply potable water while some private subdivisions have their own water distribution system. Majority have their own sanitary toilet facilities like septic tank.

Southville 3A in Barangay San Antonio is a relocation site constructed by the National Housing Authority (NHA) for the informal settlers living along the PNR railroad track and danger or hazardous area. About 2,060 families were relocated and provided with row houses measuring 20 square meters. There is problem of water supply as an upland area.

#### Social Welfare

- 1.6.10.1 Key Issues and Concerns


Family Welfare

1. Increasing Number of Street Dwellers such as families, children and adults.

Street dwellers involve a range of risks including eviction, violence, trafficking and a violation of children’s basic rights in nutrition and education. Impoverished slum dwellers living in fragile pockets of the city suffer from poor health as evidenced by the prevalence of stunting and underweight children. Most street dwellers are unable to register their children at birth which limits their lack of access to basic services including quality education. Another outcome is the prevalence of child labor. In the short term, children are exposed to a variety of hazards and worse, sexual abuse.

Women’s Welfare

- 1. Increase number of sexually abused women and children.

Sexual assaults such as rape will always be a traumatic experience to the abused that can lead to a feeling of low self-esteem, self-blame and guilt that can endure for months and years after the assault. The victim of this kind of assault may experience complexities of oneself which can lead to restriction in social activities, including work and community involvement. Sexual assault also affects partners, children, family and friends of the victim/survivor, as well as the wider community.

- 2. Increasing number of victims of R.A. 9262, otherwise known as, “The Anti Violence Act against Women and their Children of 2004.”


Physical effects of violence against women has been linked to many serious health consequences such as body injuries, disability, chronic health problems or various chronic pain syndromes, hypertension, sexual and reproductive health problems, among others.

On the psychological side, the effects may include anxiety, fear, mistrust of others, inability to concentrate, post-traumatic stress disorder, depression, suicide, withdrawal and alcohol or drug use.

On its social effects, acts of violence also affect the productivity of women. The economic impact of abuse may extend to losses in women’s earning potential as it will greatly “damaged” their confidence and their income-generating opportunities. The negative impact on the women’s self-image prevents the victims from reaching their full potential and self-realization.

Child and Youth Welfare

1. Increasing Number of Children in Conflict with the Law (RA 9344-Juvenile Justice Welfare Act)

Among the factors that children coming into conflict with the law are poverty, lack of parental care (children who cannot live at home due to economic conditions or exposure to abuse),

poor parenting or parental neglect. Poor educational attainment was also found to be a contributory factor for children coming into conflict with the law.

A proper approach to juvenile justice requires that efforts must be made to prevent children from coming into conflict with the law. This work is not just for government but for the entire society in general. Essential services, including prevention, recovery and reintegration should be in a place offering community-based and family-focused assistance so that children can achieve rehabilitation and avoid repeated offenses.

- 1. Inadequate Number of social workers that will cater to the growing needs of social services and also to meet challenges on the implementation of social welfare programs.
- 2. Limited Workstation and area to accommodate clients in need for social services.


- 1.6.10.2 Sectoral Description


Social Welfare Status of the Disadvantaged and Vulnerable Population

The social and economic hardships of life are exacting a heavy toll on the local people especially the informal sector families making them highly disadvantaged and vulnerable. Poverty, drug abuse, hunger, malnutrition, diseases, unemployment, violence, crimes, trafficking, among others, hounded these people shows the number of clients served by the CSWD.

Social Welfare Programs Pantawid Pamilya Pilipino Program (PPPP)- Conditional Cash Transfer (CCT) Program A poverty reduction strategy of the national government that provides conditional cash grants to extremely poor households to improve their health, nutrition and education particularly of children aged 0-14 year old. It provides incentives for poor families to invest in their future by ensuring that mothers and children can avail the free healthcare as well as the free education for children. As such, it is a human development program that invests in the health and education of children. 4Ps has dual objectives such as to provide cash assistance to the poor to alleviate their immediate needs and break the intergenerational poverty cycle through investments in human capital

WORKER

###### Family and Community Welfare Program

A multiple Program that includes (a) Family Drug Abuse Prevention, (b) Parents Effectiveness services, (c) Pre-Marriage Counseling Service, (d) Solo Parent Services and (e) Family Casework Services. Accomplishments for 2015 are as follows:

- a. 6,089 parents underwent training/seminar regarding their responsibilities and rightful caring for their children and family.
- b. 3,852 couples underwent pre-marriage seminar as a requirement for their intended marriage to enable them to carry the responsibility and challenges of a married life.
- c. 222 solo parents were issued IDs to claim the benefit and services of the Solo Parents Act.
- d. 13 married couples underwent Family Counseling Program to address problems encountered in child rearing.


###### Women’s Welfare Program

Program that promotes the welfare of women giving special attention to prevention in the exploitation of women in any forms, including prostitution, illegal recruitment as well as promotion of child for employment and self-actualization. Accomplishments for 2015 are as follows:

- a. 5 Women were assisted and given the chance to fight for their rights under R.A. 9262 (Violence against Women and Children)
- b. 8,480 women received seminar and information regarding laws involving women’s rights.


###### Child and Youth Welfare Program

Program that provides care, protection and rehabilitation of children and youth. It includes neglected children, out of school youth, street children, children in difficult circumstances, victims of any forms of abuse and exploitation and children in conflict with the law. Accomplishments for 2015 are as follows:

- a. 51 day care centers are being supported by the City Gov’t.
- b. 53 persons were employed as Day Care Workers to sustain the needs of the program
- c. 3,170 children in the day Care Service Program under the supervision of the CSWD received continuous provision of funds from the local government
- d. 4 complete sets of Day Care Institutional materials were provided for the proposed computerized training system
- e. 74 children in conflict with the law had been under the care and custody of the CSWD. These children and their parents were evaluated and have undergone counseling. Some were referred to government institutions depending on the degree of cases. They are also under the diversion programs that were facilitated and supervised by the barangay authorities and the CSWD.


###### Senior Citizens Programs

Program that caters to the needs of the senior citizens of the city with the issuance senior citizens cards and purchase booklets to avail of the mandated discounts as provided for by the law. The program also provides for social pension that gives cash assistance to indigent

elderly to augment their daily subsistence and other medical needs. Accomplishments for 2015 are as follows:

- a. 4,186 new members who applied were given senior citizens ID card for discounts in groceries, medicines, agriculture and other benefits due for the seniors. As of December 2015, there are 30,545 registered senior citizens in this city.
- b. 325 indigent senior citizens 77 years old and above who are frail, sickly and disabled benefited from this the social pension program.


###### Programs for Persons with Disability

Program that caters to the needs of the Persons with Disability (PWD) in relation to the issuance of the PWD identification cards giving cardholders mandated discounts as provided for by law. It also includes issuances of purchase booklets.

a. As of December 2015, there are 779 registered PWDs in the city. Emergency Assistance Program

Program that gives assistance to individuals in crisis situation such as counseling services, medical assistance, transportation assistance, burial assistance, educational assistance, emergency or shelter assistance. Accomplishments for 2015 are as follows:

- a. 19,400 recipients were given medical and burial assistance.
- b. 3,579 were given educational assistance
- c. 21 recipients were aided via the Balik-Probinsya Program
- d. 13,780 patients were assisted through coordination with different agencies including the PCSO and PAGCOR

### Infrastructure, Roads, Transport and Utilities

![image 137](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile137.png)

#### Key Issues and Concerns

- 1. Very restricted road infrastructure in Magsaysay and Narra Road in terms of width, lack of drainage, observance of setbacks, sidewalks, parking areas, among others, discouraging business investments
- 2. Reliable public transport facilities needed in the upland Barangays
- 3. Need to efficiency connect San Pedro to Dasmariñas and Daang Hari, Bacoor in Cavite through road and transport facilities
- 4. Need to formulate a master plan to develop the large raw land areas in the upland for mixed use development
- 5. Increasing number of colorum vehicles contributing to traffic congestion
- 6. Need to study the long term impact of growing population in terms of traffic congestion, road infrastructure, mass transit system, potable water supply, among others
- 7. Need to improve the delivery of potable water supply particularly in the upper villages
- 8. Need for local radio broadcasting station
- 9. Need to install backbone telecom facilities like fiber optic to encourage the IT BPO Industry
- 10. Increase the capacity of waste to energy technology like methane gas extraction in the landfill areas
- 11. Need to address the treatment of waste water, septage and storm water
- 12. Need to do master planning to utilize Laguna Lake portion of San Pedro for mixed use development
- 13. Need to formulate a master plan for the urban renewal of San Pedro
- 14. Lack of northbound access to the SLEX
- 15. Improvement of the drainage system infrastructures based on a comprehensive study and master planning.
- 16. For reason of aesthetic and safety, utility lines need to go underground like telephones, fiber optic lines, and cable TV, others provided by service utility providers
- 17. Need to acquire land and construct buildings for social support and administrative support infrastructure facilities like health centers, schools, socialized housing, jail, firefighting, parks, assembly areas, among others
- 18. Need to capacitate the planning and engineering offices in project planning, evaluation and monitoring of programs and projects. Roads and Bridges

Drainage System

With the completion of the Comprehensive Study and Master Planning of the Integrated Drainage System, there will be a concerted effort to address the flash flooding problem occurring in the various areas of San Pedro with the construction of flood control and drainage infrastructures.

![image 139](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile139.png)

#### Transport Mobility and Access

###### Internal Circulation and External Linkages

The Old National Highway traversing the Laguna Cities and Municipalities provides the main access of San Pedro where the daily traffic volume is medium to heavy used by all types of vehicles, public and private. Public utility vehicles include buses, jeepneys, AUVs and tricycles. There are bus terminals along the National Highway catering to passengers going to and from Metro Manila.

It was observed that there are not enough public utility vehicles going to the upper villages at the inconvenience of the residents going to their place of work, schools and establishments. A big issue is the increasing number of colorum vehicles such as jeepneys and tricycles of about 30 percent. These unregistered vehicles contribute much to the traffic problem while not paying franchise tax to the government.

To and from Metro Manila, the Old National Highway and the SLEX are the most direct routes although there are 10 entry and exit points in San Pedro, namely:

- 1. Old National Highway leading to Biñan
- 2. Old National Highway leading to Muntinlupa
- 3. SLEX leading to Muntinlupa and Calamba
- 4. SLEX leading to Metro Manila
- 5. Magsaysay Road leading to Victoria Homes/DaangHari
- 6. Cuyab Street leading to Tunasan, Muntinlupa
- 7. F. Amorsolo leading to Biñan
- 8. Macaria Avenue leading to Biñan
- 9. Pacita 4th Street leading to Biñan
- 10. UB leading to GMA, Cavite


###### Water Transport in Laguna Lake

Eventually, Laguna Lake will be used by commuters and for cargo around the communities in the Provinces of Rizal and Laguna and the Metro Manila areas. Definitely, it would have positive economic, social and environmental impacts. There will be navigation lanes for ferry boats regulated by the LLDA and terminals/ports would have to be established in strategic areas feasible for operations. San Pedro is an ideal spot for the establishment of port/terminal either along the seashores of Barangay San Roque and Landayan. There is a need to conduct a pre- feasibility study to determine the technical and financial aspects particularly the joint venture arrangements between the LLDA, San Pedro and the private sector operator. Water transport is part of the inter- modal transport system recommended by transportation experts to alleviate heavy land traffic gridlock and energy consumption.

###### Map 9. Existing Road and Transport Network

![image 140](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile140.png)

In the future, the major road network and transport infrastructure will no longer be able to cope with the spiking number of public utility vehicles, company shuttle buses, private cars and motorbikes. The level of congestion will surely increase over the years during the process of rapid population growth and urbanization with social and economic consequences.

To address these concerns, there is a need for modern traffic engineering and management system such as more overpasses, foot bridges and flyovers in the likes of Metro Manila along the National Highway. There should be road widening to accommodate different types of mass transport modes such as mini buses. Road setbacks should be strictly enforced for residential and commercial establishments. Traffic lights and signage are needed in traffic prone areas. The conducts of traffic impact study for major business establishments with mitigation measures are needed.

In particular, is the need to improve the restricted Magsaysay and Narra Roads going to the upland areas connected to the commercial business establishments of DaangHari and Dasmariñas, Cavite. If converted into National Road from Provincial Road category, the DPWH will be in a position to undertake its widening, sidewalks recovery and drainage canals improvement.

Magsaysay Road has a total length of 2.47 km with a width of 10 meters used by motorists from nearby Barangays. Public utility vehicles and 814 tricycles are plying the routes. For lack of sidewalks, pedestrians walk along the road obstructing the flow of traffic. Sidewalk areas are used as extension areas of residence as garden and commercial business and for parking. Illegal vendors used them. Business establishments and informal settler’s families occupy the road right of way (ROW) and setback requirements.

![image 141](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile141.png)

#### Water Supply

San Pedro is being served by 3 waterworks system namely:

- 1. San Pedro Water District in joint venture with Prime Water (SPWD) with 623,729 cubic meters of water per month.
- 2. San Pedro Resettlement Area Water Service Cooperative Inc. (SPRACI)
- 3. The private deep wells that service the upper village residents


The SPWD, SPRACI/LABEDA has a total of 29 pumping stations stationed in localities serving the following Barangays:

- 1. BagongSilang 7. Laram 13. San Antonio
- 2. Cuyab 8. Magsaysay 14. UB
- 3. Estrella 9.Poblacion 15. Riverside
- 4. UBL 10. Nueva 16. Sampaguita
- 5. Landayan 11. San Vicente 17. Calendola
- 6. Langgam 12. Sto. Niño 18. Narra


![image 142](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile142.png)

#### Power Supply

MERALCO is the only electricity distributor in San Pedro with about 98 percent of household served. The biggest consumers are households, followed by industrial, commercial and street lights. Electricity is provided 24/7 with no brownout and blackout. There is methane gas extraction converted into electricity with capacity of 4 megawatts in the upland near the sanitary landfill areas.

![image 143](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile143.png)

#### Telecommunications

Postal services are being handled by the Bureau of Posts with main office in Pacita.Telegraphic services provided by the Bureau of Communication. Land line, cell phone and broadband services are provided by PLDT, Digitel, Globe, Sun and Smart. Cable TV is available through Skycable, Cablelink and Cignal. Fibre optic lines should be installed along potential areas ideal for IT BPO operations. National and local newspapers are widely

available. Major bank are operating and transacting foreign exchange while credit cards are accepted.

![image 144](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile144.png)

#### Sports, Recreational and Convention Facilities

San Pedro needs a modern, big capacity convention and sports center fitted for a new City although there is a medium scale stadium owned by the city of local standards. There are a number of basketball courts in every Barangays and open spaces in private subdivisions used for public assembly. There is a need for more pocket parks for greenery and recreation.

![image 145](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile145.png)

#### Burial Grounds

It can be assumed that given the low death rate, the current cemeteries and memorial parks of San Pedro are capable of accommodating the expected dead over the years. It should be emphasized that current available land area for burial is privately owned while the current public cemetery can be expanded through bone crypt construction and the use of crematorium can be advocated. The city government initiated steps to address the foregoing by developing a middle class type cemetery located at SitioRustan, Brgy. Langgam. The first phase of development is roughly 90%. Inauguration is slated within the month of May, 2016 and the pre-selling activities will start as soon as the HLURB granted the LGU the License to Sell.

See Map 13 showing the location of government facilities like Schools, Health Centers, Hospital, Police Stations, Fire Stations, New City Hall, Old Municipal Building, Public Plaza, Town Center, Public Market, Cemeteries and Memorial Park, Resettlement Areas, others.

![image 146](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile146.png)

### Urban Renewal

![image 147](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile147.png)

#### Key Issues and Concerns

- 1. There are decrepit and outdated physical structures that include old buildings as well as outmoded infra and utilities that must be refigured to make way to more modern, efficient and aesthetically designed ones
- 2. Idle and poorly utilized properties are not into its highest and best use with widest distribution
- 3. Enabling legislations nonexistent regulating construction, renovation, alteration or repair of properties and establishments or infra works in the urban renewal areas
- 4. Historical and religious areas for cultural and tourism development are not master planned with the standards and guidelines to include not only the design of buildings but also specifications on the design of sidewalks, lamp posts, walls, park benches, street signs, waiting sheds, pot planters and garbage bins
- 5. Successful urban renewal program requires good leadership, long term economic strategy, immense institutional capacity, well financed infra, high quality education and constant pursuit towards design excellence, a big challenge to the political leadership of the City Government.
- 6. On the short term, there are highly visible area that needs beautification and landscaping like along major thoroughfares, railroad tracks and near bodies of water while green pocket parks can be identified for development in various areas.


#### Urban Renewal Areas for Master Planning Religious Areas for Tourism

![image 148](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile148.png)

This area covers Barangay Landayan where the Shrine of Lolo Uweng is located extending towards the National Highway visited by thousands of Christian pilgrims every Friday and during the Lenten Season. The Barangay Road from the National Road going to the Shrine should be the object of urban renewal to include residential, commercial and institutional establishments as well as the designs of sidewalks, lamp posts, benches, signage, pot planters, others. With the religious icon, the religious celebration can be developed and elevated into a national and global class status for tourism.

![image 149](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile149.png)

#### Laguna Lake Waterfront Areas for Mixed Use

This covers the coastal Barangays fronting Laguna Lake as prime property for mixed used development. Henceforth, the false notion of the Lake as the backyard of the coastal areas should be corrected by these new frontiers of waterfront development. Developments can be modular either on stilts or reclamation starting from a small area of several hectares but expandable to hundred hectares to achieve desired economies of scale in terms of development costs and operations. An appropriate master planning, market study and financial – business planning under joint venture arrangement between the government and the private sector should be undertaken

![image 150](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile150.png)

#### Upland Green Areas for Business Parks and Recreation

In the upland area of Barangay San Antonio traversed by Magsaysay Road, there are still big pockets of privately owned land of at least 100 hectares that can be consolidated and developed for mixed use such as business park, recreation and high end residential enclave. Said areas can be financially packaged for market study and business planning under joint venture arrangement. The areas are strategically linked to the DaangHari, Bacoor and Dasmariñas of Cavite Province.

![image 151](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile151.png)

#### Traditional Old Town Poblacion Area

In the course of time, the old traditional poblacion areas on the north eastern side aged through the years and in danger of decay and deterioration if not planned for aged through the years and in danger of decay and deterioration if not planned for urban renewal. In the areas can be found the Spanish design Roman Catholic Church of Saint Peter, the historical public plaza, public market and the first municipal hall which are slowly fading as original center of community activities being overtaken by rapid commercialization. It should be candidate for historical redevelopment as an old district of San Pedro.

![image 152](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile152.png)

#### Beautification, Landscaping and Pocket Parks

In the short term period, there is a need to identify areas for beautification, tree planting and landscaping such as along major thoroughfares, railroad tracks and near bodies of water to break the eyesores images of said areas. There are small open spaces that can be developed into pocket parks of shrubs and trees with artistic monuments pleasing to the residents and commuters in an urban environment. The city needs directional signs that can be made tourist and travelers friendly with artistic designs also beautifying the urban areas of interest like shopping areas, restaurants, bus terminals, churches, others. In the open spaces of subdivisions, urban agriculture can be promoted as green areas for vegetable farming. Encroachment along sidewalks should be done away with for smooth flow of pedestrians.

### Institutional Development

![image 164](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile164.png)

#### Key Issues and Concerns

- 1. Preparation of plantilla positions and filling up of positions with qualified staff/workers preferably with civil service eligibility.
- 2. Need to orient, motivate and develop the work ethic of the workers for greater productivity, efficiency and effectiveness.
- 3. Need to upgrade the financial management information system linking Budget, Treasury and Accounting integrating their financial records on income and expenditures.
- 4. Need to upgrade the Geographic Information System (GIS) for tax mapping on real property and business manned by trained technical people for increased tax collection.
- 5. Unresolved Barangay disputes resulting in inaccurate income sources.
- 6. Computerization of priority offices like Human Resources Office, SP Office, others, using appropriate software and hardware for data based management.
- 7. Updating of the official website to include on-line downloadable forms for convenience of clients, savings in time and costs of personnel services.
- 8. Need to appoint head of the Management Information System.
- 9. Improvement of the services of the Public Information Office such as SUSI, LED Board and social media, AVP focused on heritage and heroes, local products and historical milestones.
- 10. Updating and posting of the process flow of the Citizen’s Charter for greater efficiency in the delivery of front line services.
- 11. Simplifying the procurement process and procedures avoiding delays in the delivery of supplies and materials and services contracting.
- 12. Improving the storage and handling of engineering supplies and materials preventing losses and pilferages.
- 13. Proper storage, warehousing and handling of used materials for use in later activities.
- 14. Establishing central file records, storage and retrieval especially for important documents using back up system like micro filming.
- 15. Delayed preparation of budget requirements such as Annual Procurement Plan (APP), observance of Commission on Audit (COA) rules.
- 16. Manual Accounting System results in slow process of reconciliation, validation and tracking of subsequent payments.
- 17. Proper alignment of the Executive Agenda with the Sangguniang Panlungsod legislative functions such as the enactment of relevant codes, enacted legislations, others.
- 18. Computerizing the operations of the SangguniangPanlungsod like the tracking of proposed legislations, classification, indexing and retrieval ordinances, resolutions and minutes.
- 19. Inaccuracies in the data base of NGO profile which require updating and centralization of data base.


###### 1.9.1.1 Sectoral Description

San Pedro became a city by virtue of Republic Act 10420 enacted on March 27, 2013. The Charter provides for the creation of the City of San Pedro composed of 21 barangays but three newly created cities have been added in 2015. In 2015, the City has a total of 340 permanent employees and 1 temporary and 744 job order workers. A new modern 5-storey building was constructed in 2009. However occupancy of the said building was done in 2011 in a private land donated by the Sibulo family.

The organizational structure of the Executive Office and Legislative Office are shown in Figure 18.

In 2015, the city government has a total income of Php875, 215, 896.21 with high collections from Business Tax and License (BTL) followed by tax income from Real Property Tax (RPT). Sooner or later, San Pedro will reach the Billion Pesos income joining the ranks of the Cities of Biñan, Santa Rosa, Cabuyao and Calamba.

Internal Revenue Allotment reached Php520,470,518.00 million in 2015 with an IRA dependency ratio of 54 percent.

It has borrowing capacity to avail of loans from GFIs. GIS technology is applied for increased revenue generation especially in tax mapping for real property and business establishments. It has a tax collection efficiency of 7.3% percent in RPT and 19% in BTL that needs improvement.

The expenditures of the city shows increasing trend over the years in terms of Personal Services, MOOE and Capital Outlay as shown in Table 61. This would indicate high levels of services provided to the local constituents such as social, economic, environmental and infrastructures.

Tax Amnesty is given to property owners delinquent in the payment of their RPT. Business One Stop Shop (BOSS) is activated January of every year to facilitate increased payment and collection of taxes and the renewal of business license.

With corporate powers, the city operates economic enterprises such as the Town Center, Wet Market, Suki Market, Cemetery and Abattoir funded by locally generated income and Government Financial Institution (GFIs). The city has no subsidiary corporations to pursue its economic agenda. City franchise is given to tricycle and jeepney operators. The city gives recognition to the top tax paying 100 business corporations.

The Water District is operating independently with its own Board of Directors, income collections and sets of officials created and guided by LWUA rules and regulations. The City funds the operations of the PUP Branch in San Pedro, the San Pedro Technical Institute, two Community Hospital and health centers in various barangays.

The PNP, BFP and BJMP are being provided with supplementary financial and logistical assistance. There is a Public Order and Safety Office that maintain the traffic system, fire protection and other auxiliary services.

There are ad hoc bodies and committees provided for by the Local Government Code such as City Development Council, Local School Board, HealthBoard, Housing Board, among

others. In zoning administration, the city can issue development permit, locational clearance as well as building permit for individual persons and private business sector. The Comprehensive Land Use DevelopmentPlan, Zoning Ordinance and Comprehensive Development Plan guides and regulate the development patterns of the City.

The SangguniangPanlungsod willenact and make legislations on various matters particularly the codes on traffic, environment, taxation, health, sanitation, and housing, among others. It can accredit Non- Government Organizations (NGOs) and People’s Organization (POs) to participate in the city government activities, programs and projects.

The City has been awarded Seal of Good Housekeeping in 2013, Seal of Good Housekeeping in 2014, and Seal of Good Local Governance from the DILG in recognition of its yearly performance. A Citizens Charter has been formulated and implemented in March 27, 2013 to guide the city government officials and the clientele of the City Government in the efficient and effective delivery of front line services. Very soon, City Government will be ISO 2001 Certified for quality management.

### Regional and Provincial Development Perspectives

![image 168](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile168.png)

#### Calabarzon Regional Development Plan, 2011-2016

The Calabarzon Development Framework is defined by the Vision and the Medium Term Goals and Development Outcomes for the Region. The vision and goals are to be achieved through the implementation of the Calabarzon Spatial Development Strategy and supplemented by distinct sectoral approaches that will be applied in the Region for 20112016 planning period.

The development of Calabarzon is anchored on the long term vision that by the first quarter of the new millennium, the region would:

-  Be the leading global economic hub of the country.
-  Be a model, livable industrial region, highly urbanized with well-planned own clusters ideal for a sustainable living.
-  Have a vibrant countryside offering an excellent alternative place to live, work and seek recreation.
-  Have a modern intermodal transportation system and digital infrastructure integrating the region within, the country and to the global community.
-  Be complemented by high quality social service amenities accessible region wide.
-  Have a citizenry whose high creativity and competitive spirit, genuine concern for the environment and fellowmen and love of country is inspired by their affinity to the region’s heroes and unwavering faith in God.


For the plan period, the Regional Development Plan shall work for the realization of the administration’s social contract with the Filipino people in which the overarching goal is to alleviate poverty. Towards this end, the plan envisions the attainment of the following outcomes:

-  High and sustained economic growth
-  Equal access to development opportunities
-  Effective social safety nets


Specifically, the success of the plan shall be assessed based on the following targets:

-  Sustainable economic growth of 5 to 8 percent annually which shall enable the economy to generate employment.
-  Maintain a single digit unemployment level.
-  Achieve the MDG targets by 2015 focusing on targets with low probability of achievement.


###### Spatial Development Strategy

One of the critical means by which the region’s vision, goals and development outcomes could be achieved is the implementation of the CALABARZON Spatial Development Strategy. The overall strategy employs the Center/Cluster-Corridor-Wedge (CCW) planning approach which seeks to direct and stimulate development and growth from the west

to east as well as to strengthen north-south development and access corridors.

In Laguna, the Center will be in Calamba City while the Corridors are in San Pedro, Biñan, Santa Rosa, Cabuyao, Los Baños, Bay, Santa Cruz and San Pablo. The Wedges are in other municipalities.

The center shall be further developed to enhance their roles as administrative centers and hubs of economic activities for industries, commerce, finance and services. The Corridors shall be developed along the existing major transport routes in the Region. Wedges shall be grouped for form smaller, functional clusters to facilitate planning and implementation of programs and projects with focus on agri-modernization and settlements development.

The coastal areas of Laguna Lake, Taal Lake and the east coast will be strengthened and their potentials for waterfront urban development, tourism and transport maximized. Laguna Lake is proposed to become a water transport corridor to link the west to the east and the north to thesouth. Laguna Lake can also serve as a hydro-technology production zone where certain areas will be will be reserved for innovative lake water treatment that will produce potable water to surrounding LGUs.

The areas surrounding the Lake can become master planned waterfront urban developments to include housing, commerce, trade, tourism and other urban land uses. The Lake shall be treated as a “front door of development” rather than the “back door”. Waterfront development would engender appreciation for the water that sustains life through the integration of water reservoir under the walkways, promenades and parks, with dikes around the lake to store huge volumes of water supply. While at the same time, walk and bike friendly areas are promoted within the lakeshore communities; a world class urban waterfront development similar to Lake Michigan, River Seine, and Lake Geneva.

Source: Calabarzon Regional Development Plan, 2011 – 2016, Calamba City, March 2011

![image 169](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile169.png)

#### Laguna de Bay Basin Master Plan, 2010 and Beyond

The Master Plan’s theme is “Towards Climate Resiliency and Sustainable Development” Medium Term Development Directions are the following:

-  Intensify waste and pollution control towards through adaptive waste management systems primarily for domestic and agricultural sources;
-  Rationalize water use zoning to harmonize all uses within the Lake including navigation and water related waste management infrastructures.
-  Promote security of lakeshore communities from flooding, health risks and minimize economic displacement through effective flood control programs and appropriate resettlement programs.
-  Rationalize watershed and shore land specific management policies based on validated threshold and vulnerability to sustain their ecological functions focused on incentive based regulations and adaptive co management systems.
-  Rationalize service and infrastructure based network support for environmental management and sustainable economic uses of lake basin resources.
-  Rationalize eco-tourism potential as development driver and promote lake sensitive eco-tourism developments.


-  Adoption of innovative financing schemes beyond regulatory fee collections.
-  Market based instruments and PPP schemes.


Long Term Development Directions

-  Improve ecosystem based performance monitoring and data management.
-  Intensify community based and incentive focused waste management systems for lakeshore settlements.
-  Upgrade quality control for lake water suited as source of freshwater of the region and parts of Metro Manila.
-  Promote adaptive co-management systems on critical areas in the shore land consistent with rationalized water use zoning.
-  Create opportunities for sustainable livelihoods through PPP schemes suited for lakeshore and upland communities.
-  Intensify development and use regulations for highly extractive economic activities with primary focus on restoration costs.
-  Pursue measures that will create buy in culture for critical environmental protection and regulation policies.
-  Comprehensive and continuing retooling of LLDA personnel.
-  Promote PPP as means of financing major investment programs and projects.


The following are the future trends/scenarios and the remaining challenges that need to be confronted in the lake basin:

-  Land cover changes and loss of forest cover.
-  Continuing rapid urbanization and industrialization.
-  Informal settlements along river banks and lake riparian environment.
-  Development of major infrastructure projects with significant impacts in the lake basin ecosystem.


Vision 2026 for Laguna de Bay Basin

“An abundant, living, and sustainably developed lake that optimally benefits all sectors of society in the Laguna Lake Basin”.

“By 2026, an abundant, living, and sustainably developed lake that optimally benefits all sectors of society in the Laguna Lake Basin.” This vision is translated into: Watershed

 Rehabilitated and protected as water resources and sustainable source of

economic assets. Shoreland

-  Protected buffer zone
-  Secure, productive, environmentally conscious, empowered lakeshore community stakeholders.


Lakes and Rivers  Healthy ecosystem to sustain life and host complementary uses.

Scope of Policy Areas Protection and Conservation

 Areas declared for protection by existing laws and needing proactive conservation measures. These cover: remaining forest cover (reserved/production forests); central bay (including fish sanctuary); buffer zones of water bodies.

Rehabilitation

 Areas needing special and immediate rehabilitation and restoration. These cover: denuded watersheds; critical river tributaries; shore land and salvage zone (flooded areas); west bay.

Lake Sensitive Development

 Areas where sustainable economic support is needed and where LLDA can demonstrate its strategic economic investments. These cover: south and east bays; shore land green development zone; other lands suitable for mix uses.

Source: Laguna de Bay Basin Master Plan, 2010 and Beyond, Draft Final Report, Laguna Lake Development Authority, December 2015

![image 170](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile170.png)

#### Laguna Provincial Development and Physical Framework Plan, 2010 – 2020

###### Existing Land Use

The general classification of land use in Laguna is agricultural, built up and forest. Laguna devoted 60,624 hectares of the 134,720 hectares of alienable and disposable lands to agricultural use. More than half are planted to permanent crops and 38.8 percent are planted to temporary crops. The rest are permanent meadows, pastures, land covered with forest growth and lands lying idle.

Due to rapid urbanization, most of the agricultural lands are converted to residential, commercial and industrial uses especially in the cities of San Pedro, Biñan, Sta Rosa, Cabuyao, Calambaand Los Baños which are the Primary Growth Center and Secondary Growth Centers of Laguna.

Tourism and Institutional areas are located in the second district while there are an abundance of historical edifices, hot springs, delicious fruits and cuisine in the 3rd and 4th district. The 1st district has a major attraction in Enchanted Kingdom in Santa Rosa while the Makiling-Los Baños area is a major hub for nature education, outdoor campuses for the endemic flora and fauna of Mt. Makiling.

The protected areas are located in the municipalities along the mountains of Mt. Makiling, Mt. Banahaw-San Cristobal and the Sierra Madre Range. Areas in the lakeshore towns of Laguna are Network of Areas for Agricultural and Agro-Industrial Development (NPAA) highly restricted agricultural lands.

###### Physical Framework

The Province of Laguna is opting for the Agri-Industrial Strategy without neglecting its tourism industry which is one of the major earners in the provincial economy. This strategy is concentrated in developing the province agri-industrial and resource-oriented tourism potentials in the 3rd and 4th district. It aims to strengthen the harmony of agriculture and industry by increasing agricultural production and increasing the capacity of processing agri -based resources and produced into processed industrial outputs.

Inherent in the preferred strategy is the development of key urban areas to serve as the trading/commercial centers, primary and secondary industrial areas and eco-tourism centers. The strategy will bring about an increase in population due to influx of inhabitants within the urban area. As to its impact on the provincial economic structure- manufacturing, agriculture, private services and trade activities will dominate the economy. This will envision San Pedro, Santa Rosa and Calamba to become Secondary Metropolitan Centers, while Biñan and Santa Cruz will be converted to small or medium sized cities.

In 2020, the settlement pattern will be the same. Expected is the influx of population in the primary growth centers especially in the First and Second Districts in view of migration from other provinces for employment and business opportunities. Laguna by 2020 will need an additional 659.31 sq. km. of land due to increase of population of 967,698 from 2010 to 2020 and an increase in population density from 1,453.2 to 2,003.1 sq. km/person. Also, there will be more increase demand in land requirement for the industrial and commercial uses.

Source: Provincial Development and Physical Framework Plan, 2010 – 2020, Prepared by the Provincial Planning and Development Coordinating Office, Province of Laguna.

### Development Framework

![image 172](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile172.png)

Development Vision Old Vision

#### A HIGHLY URBANIZED AND SELF RELIANT COMMUNITY OF INSPIRED CITIZENRY, LIVING IN A HEALTHY ENVIRONMENT AND EFFECTIVELY GOVERNED BY HONEST AND DEDICATED PUBLIC SERVANTS.

Revised Vision

BY YEAR 2021 SAN PEDRO SHALL BECOME A SECONDARY METROPOLITAN CENTER DRIVEN BY COMMERCIAL-INDUSTRIALTOURISM DEVELOPMENT WITH HEALTHY, SAFE AND SECURE ENVIRONMENT AND SOCIETY.

![image 173](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile173.png)

Development Thrusts and Strategies

Preferred Strategy: Commercial Industrial cum Tourism

San Pedro opts for the commercial industrial development strategy without neglecting its religious and cultural tourism industry as the major concern of the local economy.

In essence, the strategy aims to modernize the commercial service sector to full potentials as they provide the largest employment and income distribution to the local residents. The industrial sector companies operating in the City will be poised for the RETENTION of their manufacturing operations, their possible capacity EXPANSION in products and services, and their DIVERSIFICATION into related production areas (RED). Religious and cultural tourism have the potential for upscaling to national if not at the global levels.

The strategy will bring about an increase in migrant population due to its nearness to the Metro Manila’s business districts. As to its impact on the City economic structure, private services and wholesale-retail trade activities as well as manufacturing will dominate the local economy.

As envisioned in the Laguna Development and Physical Framework Plan, 2010- 2020 (DPFP), San Pedro has a big role and envisions to become a Secondary Metropolitan Center linked to Metro Manila, the cities and small towns of Laguna. Sooner or later, San Pedro will become a highly urbanized city.

###### Alternative Trend Development Strategies: Full Commercialization

The trend alternative strategy is projected to go towards full commercialization. It will entail the putting up of more high end tertiary business establishments like super malls, super markets, five star hotels, gourmet restaurants, branded coffee shops and fast food outlets, banks and financial institutions, high rise condominiums, government center, religious and cultural institutions, transport, recreation and sports facilities. It would mean knowledge and creativity employment based service industries like IT BPO companies.

###### Alternative 1: Commercial Industrial

For Alternative 1, San Pedro will concentrate its efforts in improving the quality of life of its residents by adopting a well balance lifestyle of work, live and play to enjoy the fruits of modernized commercial industrial development. Whereby the manufacturing industry is retained, expanded and diversified, the commercial service sector will be stimulated for further growth.

###### Alternative 2: Religious and Cultural Tourism

Conceptually, alternative 2 would entail the development of religious and cultural historical sites San Pedro is known for such as the Santo Sepulchro Shrine (Lolo Uweng), Laurel Museum, Risen Christ Statue, St. Peter the Fisherman in the Lake, among others. It would also mean the revival of the historical milestones of San Pedro such as duck raising, balut making, sampaguita flower industry, native foods, among others.

#### Health and Hospital Services

###### Goal

To provide quality cost effective public health care, hospital, and other related services available and equally accessible to the local population. Objectives

###### Strategies

-  Equitable and timely delivery of health care and hospital services to the constituents.
-  Improve environmental health and sanitation.
-  Educate and capacitate health care target beneficiaries.
-  Gradually improve the level of health care services, manpower and facilities within the means of the city government.


###### Targets

-  Explore more cost effective ways of delivering health care and hospital services.
-  Explore ways of cooperation with private hospitals and medical doctors.
-  Intensify IEC Campaign Program.
-  Coordinate with DOH and City Government in complying with Guidelines.
-  Quantitative number of clients served
-  Qualitative services delivered to clients
-  Cost recovery in health services rendered
-  Compliance to DOH set of guidelines

#### Basic Education – DEPED

Goal To provide quality public basic education accessible to all school age population at the primary and secondary levels. Objectives

-  Improve the effectiveness of public primary and secondary education.
-  Support the developmental efforts of the city through formal and non-formal education.
-  Support the K to 12 program of DEPED.
-  Attain the highest level in school participation and completion rate and zero dropout rate.


Strategies

-  Upgrading of educational facilities and teacher manpower resource.
-  Continue curriculum enrichment.
-  Expanding the non-formal education courses such as the Alternative Learning System.
-  Improving the classroom-pupil ratio, teacher pupil ratio, textbook- pupil ratio, etc.


Targets

-  Highest level in school participation rate, completion rate and decrease in dropout rate.
-  Improved performance in math, English and science achievement tests.
-  Increased level of non-formal education graduates.

#### Tertiary Education – PUP

Goal To make tertiary education as source of professional and technical manpower needed by the local economy’s secondary and tertiary sectors. Objectives:

-  Encourage colleges and other tertiary level institutions to offer relevant degree and non- degree programs
-  Prepare the work force for the emerging voice and non-voice IT BPO industries
-  Provide financial assistance to students to priority courses
-  Encourage colleges to retool their curriculum with emphasis on positive work ethics, attitudes and values.
-  Develop entrepreneurial skills of the students.


Strategies

-  Conduct of labor market study
-  Strengthening of the job orientation and placement program through PESO.
-  Coordinate with the industries in the retooling of the college curriculum.
-  Allocate scholarship quota for priority courses.
-  Conduct job fairs, job caravans and career orientation to students.
-  Conduct feasibility in the establishment of the Community College.
-  Invite BPAP to conduct job matching on IT BPO Industries.


Targets  Percentage success in licensure board exams.  Percentage increase in voice and non-voice employment.  Percentage increase of the scholarships provided/available.
