#### Technical and Vocational Education – SPTI

Goal To produce skilled workers and middle level manpower needed by the industries in support of the local economy’s secondary and tertiary sectors. Objectives

-  Provide quality technical vocational education for wage and self-employment
-  Establish linkage with the industry sector for hands on training and placement
-  Strengthen Linkage with TESDA in the provision of technical assistance to the training center and scholarship vouchers to student trainees
-  Advocacy for the City Government’s additional budget allocation and scholarship assistance to the student trainees
-  Provide vocational career guidance to the trainees


Strategies

-  Continue improving the training courses for relevance


-  Seek companies within and outside of San Pedro for on-the-job trainees
-  Procurement of additional equipment and upgrading of trainer’s qualifications
-  Training on Wheels in all Barangays and new branches establishment
-  Creation of permanent position for trainers and creation of additional position
-  Provision of City incentives to companies involved in on- the- job- training and graduates hiring


Targets

-  Improved performance in TESDA assessment tools
-  Percentage increase in the placement of graduates
-  Increased availment of TESDA Vouchers
-  Increase in on-the-job trainees

#### Social Welfare

Goal

To alleviate the hard living conditions of the disadvantaged population particularly the informal sector families, the sexually abused children and women, elderly, physically challenged and those children, mendicants, street dwellers and youth in conflict with the law.

Objectives

-  Provide comprehensive/integrated/holistic social welfare program interventions to the disadvantaged populations.
-  Provide support to the government’s Conditional Cash Transfer Program.
-  Intervene and control the increasing number of street dwellers, mendicants and children and youth in conflict with the law.
-  Address urban poverty gripping the local people.
-  Instill moral values formation like dole out mentality and mendicancy. Strategies
-  Recruit and deploy additional licensed social workers.


-  Establish community based social welfare programs.
-  Expand the social welfare work stations to accommodate more clients.
-  Coordinate with the PNP in law enforcement and NGO’s involved in the casing of the o client population.
-  Encourage self- help and self- reliance for the disadvantaged population.
-  Extend family planning and responsible parenthood program services.


Targets

 Quantitative number of clients served by programs.  Qualitative delivery of services provided.  Conditional Cash Transfer beneficiaries

#### Population and Settlement

Goal

To manage rationally the rapid urbanization of the city given the high population growth and density unevenly distributed in a limited land area with social and economic costs and consequences.

Objectives

-  Alleviate urban congestion affecting the quality of life of the residents.
-  Control informal settler proliferation in extreme poverty.
-  Maximize the best use of limited urban land for residence, industry, institution, greenery, etc.
-  Explore the potentials of Laguna Lake for mixed use development.
-  Undertake urban renewal activities.
-  Manage sustainable population growth


Strategies

-  Make use of the zoning ordinance to control and regulate urban sprawl and congestion.
-  Conduct study on the carrying capacity of the urban land area, need for potable water, power, utilities and the road and transport infrastructures.
-  Coordinate with LLDA in the mixed use development of Laguna Lake.
-  Conduct master planning of urban renewal areas.


Targets

-  Quantitative control of informal settler family proliferation
-  Quantitative control of idle and eyesore properties –land and structures, abandoned factory sites, commercial buildings, warehouse, others.
-  Construction of more vertical buildings and structures.
-  Zoning ordinance violations pertaining to best use of land.

Legislative Requirements

- 1. Updating the Zoning Ordinance provisions.
- 2. Imposing strict regulation of informal settler family proliferation.
- 3. MOA with LLDA re the mixed use of Laguna Lake.
- 4. Appropriating funds for master planning activities of urban land use redevelopment.


###### Programs and Projects

- 1. Feasibility Study and Master Planning of Urban Land
- 2. Inventory of Urban Land Utilization
- 3. Carrying Capacity Study of Urban Land for Future Land Use.
- 4. Intensive program on family planning and responsible parenthood.


![image 181](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile181.png)

#### Housing and Resettlement

Goal To provide appropriate housing assistance to the low income groups especially the informal settler families who are living in hazardous areas. Objectives

-  Provide affordable economic housing to the low income workers/residents of the city.
-  Provide relocation and resettlement areas outside of San Pedro.
-  Control and regulate the proliferation of informal sector families by the Barangays.
-  Ensure availability of basic utilities and facilities to every household in the city.


Strategies

-  Coordinate with national government agencies in the provision of socialized housing for the informal sector.
-  Secure public properties from the informal settlers.
-  Promote small family size thru family planning.
-  Intensive IEC campaign to stop proliferation of informal sector families.
-  Undertake land banking
-  Allocate annual funds for land acquisition, shelter and resettlement.
-  Undertake livelihood and skills training


Targets  Quantitative number of informal settler families provided shelter assistance.  Control of informal sector families’ proliferation in barangays.  Quantitative number of economic housing units constructed.

Legislative Requirements  Ordinance creating and strengthening Barangay Anti Squatting Task Force  Update the Zoning Ordinance about Illegal occupancy of properties  Ordinance allocating certain percentage of annual budget for land banking,

economic housing and resettlement Programs and Projects(please refer to Figure 13)

-  Economic and Socialized Housing Program
-  Land Banking Program
-  Feasibility Study in the Integrated Delivery of Basic Services (health, skills training, family planning, livelihood, balik-probinsya, etc.) for the socialized housing beneficiaries.

#### Peace and Order – PNP, BFP, BJMP and POSO

Goal To secure and make safe all residents and business community from criminal elements and fire hazards through preventive and proactive measures. Objectives

-  Harness the resources and capabilities of the PNP, BFP and BJMP in accordance with its mandate and functions.
-  Develop the capabilities of the local community as partners of the PNP and BFP in crime and fire prevention as a force multiplier.
-  Acquire needed personnel, facilities and equipment for the PNP, BFP and BJMP for efficient and effective delivery of protective services.


Strategies

-  Provision of logistical support by the city government.
-  Establishing effective networking cooperation with the local community and non- government organizations.
-  Conduct of IEC campaigns
-  Coordination with the PNP, BFP and BJMP at the national and regional levels.
-  Continuing capacity building, drills and exercises


Targets  Reduction of crime incidence overtime.  Prevention of fire incidence.  Detainees rehabilitation

#### Youth Development

Goals To promote and protect the physical, moral, spiritual, intellectual and social wellbeing of the youth sector of the City of San Pedro

Objectives

- 1. Health – For the youth to participate in the planning, implementation, monitoring, and
- 2. evaluation of child and youth-friendly programs, policies, and activities on environmental protection, climate change adaptation, and mitigation, and DRMM, with the use of new technologies and indigenous knowledge, so as to promote sustainable natural ecosystem and smarter localities, biodiversity conservation, waste management and pollution control.


- 2. Governance – For the youth to participate in governance through the involvement in local and national politics, and the government bureaucracy though programs, projects and activities by and for the youth
- 3. Active Citizenship – For the youth to participate in community development and nation building through meaningful, active, sustainable, productive and patriotic engagement in civil society, social mobilization, volunteerism (indicating social awareness and socio- civic mindedness) and values formation (such as having concern for others and being socially responsible)
- 4. Peace Building and Security – For the youth to participate in promoting human security, including participation in peace building and conflict prevention and management initiatives, and public order and safety efforts as well as safeguarding national integrity and sovereignty to contribute to national peace and unity.
- 5. Social Inclusion and Equity – For the youth to participate in a peaceful and just society that affords then with equal and equitable opportunity, social security and protection in all aspects of development, regardless of their gender, disability, specific needs political beliefs, ethnicity, religion and sociocultural economic status.
- 6. Education – For the youth to participate in accessible, developmental, quality and relevant formal, non-formal and informal lifelong learning anf training that prepares graduates to be globally competitive but responsive to national needs and to prepare them for the workplace and the emergence if new media and other technologies.
- 7. Health – For the youth to participate in multi-sensitive, multi sectoral, and inclusive programs, projects, activities, and services on health and health financial risk protection


– with serious consideration of nutrition, reproductive health and psychosocial concerns that promote the youth’s health and well being as well as to address their sexual and non-sexual risk-taking behaviours.

- 8. Economic Empowerment – For the youth to participate in the economy as empowered employees and/or entrepreneurs who are productively engaged in gainful and decent work and/or businesses and protected from work-related vulnerabilities such as discrimination, underemployment, undue contractualization, occupation hazards and child labor.


Strategies

- 1. Incorporation of the primary objectives of the City Youth and Sports Development Office with the projects, programs and activities of the different community-based youth organizations and Sangguniang Kabataan Councils.
- 2. Close coordination with the provincial youth development affairs office and other youth- serving organizations and agencies for potential project partnerships
- 3. Upgrading of facilities that can provide support to various youth development and sports activities.


Targets

- 1. Highest participation rate of all community-based youth organization and Sangguniang kabataan Councils in all the City Youth and Sports Development Office’s projects, programs and city-wide activities.
- 2. Minimization or total elimination of the number of youths involved in illegal and improper activities.
- 3. Improved performance in all the centers of youth participation

### Environmental Management, Climate Change Adaptation and Disaster Risk Mitigation

###### Goal

To secure and make safe all residents and business community addressing environmental management, climate change adaptation and instituting disaster mitigation measures.

###### Objectives

· Prevent and protect the city from hazards and vulnerabilities such as inland flash flooding, earthquake seismicity, lakeshore flooding, ground subsidence, air pollution, solid waste and waste water pollution, landslides, among others.

· Undertake capacity building to prepare the residents and respond effectively and efficiently from natural and man - made disasters and calamities. · Network with national government agencies and private sectors before, during and after the occurrence of disasters and calamities.

· Institute environmental management measures on liquid and solid wastes as well as air pollution and climate change proofing of the city both structural and non structural measures.

###### Strategies

· Stop development in high hazard areas if not possible minimize land use

intensity, buildings value and occupancy. · Mitigate risks through urban design, site planning and building construction. · Protect life and existing development through evacuation plans, redevelopment

and retrofitting of built up communities. · Future developments should not disturb protective environmental systems and not create conditions that contribute to risks. · Plan for hazard prone areas in Laguna Lakeshore, river systems, and flash flood prone interior areas.

· Widening and dredging of the waterways, improve the urban drainage system intakes and outfalls, and explore the establishments of detention ponds and rain harvesting technology.

· Retention of specific green areas for open spaces and parks in every barangays as buffer.

· Monitoring and regulation in the extraction of ground water, promotion of green technology for energy efficiency and water conservation in houses, office and factory buildings.

· Other than MRF, cutting edge technology on waste energy that needs no sanitary landfill shall be explored.

· Disaster rescue, evacuation and relief assistance will be done for people affected by flooding, landslides and earthquakes seismic movements including the establishment of permanent evacuation centers, capacity building and drills/exercises at the barangay level as well as schools, public and private establishments.

Targets · River dredging, slope protection, tree planting and solid waste dumping control. · Smoke emission and noise pollution control. · Drainage system improvement. · Waste water and sewage control. · IEC program, drills, exercises conducted. · Monitoring and inspections of ground and surface water. · Inspections of residential and business establishments for violations. · Removal of informal sector families near bodies of water. · Zero casualty in disaster. · Rescue, evacuation and relief assistance provided to victims of disasters.

Legislative Requirements

- 1. Update Zoning Ordinance to address vulnerabilities and hazards.
- 2. Enact legislations related on Disaster Mitigation and Climate Change Adaptation
- 3. Provide Incentives/Awards/Recognitions on Climate Change Adaptation
- 4. Enact the Environmental and Climate Change Adaptation Code


Programs and Projects

- 1. River dredging, slope protection, drainage improvement, flood control Project
- 2. Smoke emission and noise pollution control Project.
- 3. Septage Treatment Project
- 4. Monitoring and Inspection of ground water extraction Program
- 5. IEC Program
- 6. Tree Planting Program
- 7. Liquid and Solid Waste Program
- 8. Compliance monitoring and inspection of residential and business establishments on climate change adaptation, environmental code, disaster risk mitigation, zoning ordinance and building code.
- 9. Conduct of capacity building program.
- 10.Establishment of permanent evacuation centers.

### Economic Development Plan

###### Goal

To revitalize and boost the commercial and industrial sector focused on the IT-BPO industry, the MSME, tertiary sector activities like trade, banking, finance, real estate, services, the government economic enterprises, urban agriculture, religious tourism, logistics, among others.

Objectives · To promote the IT BPO industry · To enhance the MSMEs · To enhance the tertiary sector activities · To improve the performance of the government economic enterprises · To promote urban agriculture · To enhance the religious and cultural tourism industry · To promote the logistics industry · To promote the health and wellness industry

###### Strategies

· Implementation of the Investment Code with fiscal and non-fiscal incentives in

addition to the BOI and PEZA investment promotion. · Development of new Business Parks for mixed use. · Explore the advantages Public Private Partnership or Joint Venture for

government and private sector projects. · Implement Urban Renewal Program · Coordinate with national government agencies like BOI and PEZA in attracting

foreign investment. · Convince the property owners, big and small, to participate and become partners

in the development of new business parks and urban renewal program. · Establishment of Investment Promotion Office. · Implement the policy on retention, expand and diversify (RED) for business and

industry.

Targets · Increased number of new industrial companies. · Entry of IT BPO companies and buildings. · Retention, expansion and diversification of companies. · New MSMEs establishment. · Increased earning capacity of economic enterprises. · Employment absorption of residents. · Quantitative monitoring and profiling of religious tourists

###### Table 111. Sector Analysis: Local Economy

### Infrastructure and Land Use Development Plan

###### Goal

To make infrastructure facilities like the roads system, transportation network and public utilities an effective instrument in the development of the local economy, rational and best use of land, the efficient delivery of basic services, among others.

Objectives

-  Improvement of the access road infrastructures in the upland areas in terms of road widening, setbacks compliance, drainage improvement, sidewalks construction, among others.
-  Linking San Pedro to the rapidly developing areas of Bacoor and Dasmariñas, Cavite.
-  Mixed used development of the remaining tracks of under-developed lands in the upland and low land areas identified in the CLUP thereby creating new opportunities for private sector investments in business parks, high end residential communities, recreational and other social amenities.
-  Establish an efficient public transport system to service the upland areas connected to the Province of Cavite.


-  ·Ensure the provision of an efficient network of services in water supply, telecommunication facilities and electric power distribution.

-  Utilize the strict implementation and compliance to the Zoning Ordinance as a tool for the best use of land, control ISF proliferations, among others.
-  Undertake capacity building for city government staffs on project development, implementation, evaluation and monitoring of projects.
-  Undertake the construction of the Northbound Exit of San Pedro in the South Luzon Expressway while structural audit and retrofitting of bridges along SLEX should be undertaken.
-  Determine the long term impact of growing population in land use planning, traffic congestion, road infrastructure, mass transport system, water supply, waste water treatment, among others.


-  ·Advocacy to increase the capacity of the waste to energy of Bakavalley for interconnection to the Transcon Grid with incentives.


-  Acquisition of land and construction of social support services infrastructures like hospitals, schools, and public socialized housing, facilities for the disadvantaged population, drainage and sewage systems.
-  Acquisition of land and construction of administrative services like buildings for police, fire, jail, park and public assembly areas


Strategies

-  Coordinate with the national government and Congress the conversion of Magsaysay and Narra Roads as well as other provincial roads into national road to make sure that road improvements will be undertaken by the DPWH.
-  Identification and start the acquisition of Right of Way (ROW) areas that will be needed in the new construction and improvements of the road networks.
-  Negotiate with the big private sector investors the development of the underdeveloped green areas in the upland and low land through master and business planning in joint venture with owners of the land.
-  Coordinate and cooperate with the Local Government Units of Cavite the opening and improvement of the access road between San Pedro and Cavite.
-  Coordinate with the water supply entities the efficient and adequate provision of potable water in the water scarce areas.


 ·Coordinate with the telecommunication companies the installation of

backbone broadband facilities like fiber optic lines for use by the IT BPO companies.

-  Coordinate with national government agencies like NEDA, DAP, DPWH, others in the conduct of capacity building and skills development program.
-  Determine the road, transport and utility needs of the urban renewal projects
-  Coordinate with the service provider ways and means of putting underground utility lines of telephones, cable TV, others.


Targets  Construction of support and administrative services buildings.  Road improvement in kilometers.  Traffic Engineering and Management Systems Improvement.  Zoning Ordinance Implementation and Compliance.  Underground provision of utility lines.  Control of colorum vehicles.  Flood control and slope protection  Drainage improvement

### Urban Renewal Development Plan

###### Goal

To revitalize and bring new life to the city landscape in terms of master planning and redeveloping the religious and historical areas, lake waterfront areas, green areas in the upland and traditional old town poblacion areas.

###### Objectives

-  Refiguring the decrepit and outdated physical infrastructures to make way for more modern, efficient and aesthetically designed ones.
-  Making idle and poorly utilized properties for highest and best use.
-  Enacting legislations regulating construction, renovation, alteration or repair of properties and establishments or infra works in urban renewal areas.
-  Master planning of historical and religious areas for cultural and tourism development with standards and guidelines


 ·Clearing, beautification, tree planting and landscaping of areas along the National Highway, railroad tracks, sidewalks, rivers and other eye sore spots including the establishment of pocket parks fitted for rapidly urbanizing city losing its greenery and beauty.

###### Strategies

-  Coordination with NGAs like National Historical Commission, National Commission on Arts and Culture, Department of Tourism, TIEZA, others in the planning and development of urban renewal programs and projects.
-  Undertake master and business planning of urban renewal programs and projects with funding from donations of the private sector.
-  Seek cooperation agreements with private sector foundations involved in urban renewal.
-  Seek cooperation with religious groups and historical society involved in urban renewal.
-  Explore possible grant technical assistance from foreign donors.
-  Establish foundation with tax free common funds from donations and contributions in the implementation of urban renewal.


###### Targets

-  Conduct of master planning activities.
-  Solicited private sector assistance, cash and in kind.
-  Conduct of IEC programs.
-  Incentives availed for urban renewal.

### Institutional Development Plan

###### Goal

To make the city government machinery highly efficient and effective as instrument in delivery of basic and frontline services, resource mobilization, income generation, legislations, among others, thereby fulfilling its avowed vision and mission.

###### Objectives

-  Ensuring workers and employees are qualified, skilled and motivated in performing their duties, responsibilities and functions.
-  Upgrading the financial management system Upgrading the GIS in tax mapping for increased tax collection.
-  Resolving the barangay boundary disputes for equitable income sharing.
-  Computerization of important services for increased efficiency.
-  Updating the official website for usefulness and relevance.
-  Appointing head of MIS
-  Improving the information program of the PAIO.
-  Upgrading dissemination system of Citizen's Charter.
-  Simplifying the procurement process.
-  Improving the storage and handling of supplies and equipment for later use.
-  Improving records filing system for storage and retrieval.
-  Timely preparation and submission of budgetary requirements.
-  Computerization of the manual accounting system.
-  Aligning the executive and legislative agenda
-  Computerization of the SP operations.
-  Accurate profiling of NGOs for SP accreditation.


###### Strategies

-  Conduct of capacity building program for workers and employees.
-  Linking the Treasury, Budget and Accounting Financial operations.
-  Use of appropriate computer software and hardware and conduct GIS training.
-  SP to enact resolution resolving Barangay disputes.
-  Conduct of research for input to the website and PIO information materials.
-  Hire qualified MIS officer
-  Updating the process and flow system of Citizen's Charter.
-  Adopt ways to simplify procurement process.
-  Use container vans for storage of supplies and materials
-  Microfilming of documents
-  Conduct orientation in the preparation of budgetary requirements like APP.
-  Use appropriate software and hardware in computerization of accounting system.
-  Improve ELA formulation.
-  Use appropriate software and hardware in the SP computerization.
-  NGOs are required to update their profiles every year.


###### Targets

-  List of qualified and eligible workers and employees.
-  Efficiency and effectiveness indicators.
-  Priority Barangay Boundary Disputes.
-  Micro Filming of Documents.
-  Storage, handling and recycling of supplies and materials.
-  Timely reconciliation of financial records and tracking of subsequent payments.
-  Timely submission of budgetary requirements.
-  Updated NGO profiles
-  Customer/client satisfaction
-  Good and effective local governance (SGLG)
-  Become a highly urbanized city
-  Increased tax collection

## PLANNING, PROGRAMMING, BUDGETING AND IMPLEMENTATION SYSTEM (PPBIS)

![image 190](912157126-San-Pedro-Comprehensive-Development-Plan_images/imageFile190.png)

### Legal Basis and Rationale of the Local Development Investment Plan (LDIP)

###### Legal Basis and Rationale of the Local Development and Investment Program (LDIP)

LDIP is the principal instrument for implementing the CDP, 2016-2022. It translated the CDP into programs and projects by selecting those that will be picked up by the City Government for funding in the annual General Fund or through Special Fund Generation Schemes.

LDIP serves as the link between the CDP and the Budget thus putting into effect the directive of the Local Government Code (RA 7160) that says “local budgets shall operationalize approved local development plans”. The three-year program is the Executive Legislative Agenda (ELA). Its annual component is referred to as the Annual Investment Program (AIP).

 Empowerment, Involvement and Commitment Model

 Plan - Budget Link Model

 Planning - Budgeting Cycle

 Plan - Budget Flow

 Development Process

- 1. Long List of Projects from the CDP, 2016-2022
- 2. LDIP short listings of projects can be prioritized through the adoption of selection criteria such as urgency, timeliness, preparedness, income generation, social acceptability, others.

-  Impact Projects, 2016-2018
-  Short Term Projects, 2019-2021
-  Enabling Legislations ( EO, SP Resolution, PDC Resolution, Congressional RA)


- 3. Shortlisted Projects for Detailed Design Development (Pre FS, Master Planning)
- 4. Funds Sourcing Menu

-  Local Budget - projected capital outlay and surplus funds from preceding year (2016 -2018).
-  National Government assistance from DBM, DPWH, DOST, DTI, TESDA, others.
-  External Assistance (Grant and Technical Assistance) – ADB/CDIA, AUSAID, USAID, JICA, others
-  GFIs – DBP, LBP, PVB
-  Private Sector Funding under PPP, BOT, JV, others


- 5. Project Implementation, Monitoring, Evaluation and Control


- 6. Capacity Building Component – Conduct of Project Development Skills Training assisted by National Government Agencies (NEDA, LGA, DAP, DPWH, DOTC, DTI, DOST, others)


Requirements

- 1. Each Sector TWG to prepare individual project profile of short listed projects, undertake action planning and conduct research and development.
- 2. Local Finance Committee composed of Treasury, Budget, Accountant, others, to make a projection of the capital outlay of the budget and the surplus funds of the preceding year. (2016-2018).
- 3. CPDO to make an inventory of national government agencies assistance and external funding sources; and mount project development skills training in cooperation with national government agencies.
- 4. Issuance of EO on Project Planning, Programming, Budgeting and Implementation System (PPBIS).


Profile of the Project/Program

- 1. Project Title
- 2. Project Rationale and Objectives
- 3. Description of Project
- 4. Estimated Cost (PS, MOOE, Capital Outlay)
- 5. Timetable of Implementation (1, 2, 3 years)
- 6. Source of Funding: Local, National, Foreign, GFI, Private Sector
- 7. Initial Assessment of Project Feasibility (Technical, Financial, Management, others)


Menu of Fund Sourcing & Technical Assistance

- 1. DPWH - Waste Water, Septage Treatment, Roads, Drainage, Slope Protection, School building
- 2. DOTC - Transport development
- 3. DOST - IT/BPO, science education, metal fabrication, renewal energy
- 4. DENR - Environmental protection
- 5. DTI - MSMEs, livelihood, IT BPO
- 6. TESDA - Voucher Training Scholarship
- 7. BOI & PEZA - Industry development, economic zones, incentives provision
- 8. TIEZA - Tourism Development
- 9. LGA/DAP/NEDA- Project development staff development
- 10. PPP Center - BOT, JV, PPP arrangement
- 11. HFC/HUDCC/NHA-Housing and resettlement
- 12. DOLE - Employment
- 13. DILG - Local government
- 14. BLGF - Local government finance
- 15. DBM - Budget assistance


- 16. DOH - Health and hospital services
- 17. DEPED - Basic education
- 18. CHED - Tertiary education
- 19. LLDA - Lakeshore development
- 20. PRA - Land reclamation
- 21. GFIs - DBP, LBP, PVB


Foreign Donors:

- 1. ADB-CDIA
- 2. EU Commission
- 3. Government of Japan & JICA
- 4. AUSAID
- 5. USAID
- 6. Others
